- [x] 2026-09-27 (session `grinder-ao-1` on kmaster) — **Done 2026-09-28 (Paul): a design round: TD-212. Until it lands, it stays refused and a rollback is a revert PR.** Was: **Decide: `ao promote --sha` (a rollback to an older commit) is refused, where design §4.7 had it. Keep it refused until the designer says how, or ask for a design round?** The promote's `run` installs the checkout's tree, and precondition (1) holds that tree at main's head, so an older commit has no way to reach `run`; the build (#666) refuses `--sha` and says why in §4.7 and the ledger. The techlead (reading #666) asked for it to be on the board, since it narrows what the design promised. A rollback today is a revert merged to main and then a press. Default if you say nothing: it stays refused, and a rollback is a revert PR. Context: TD-132 / PR #666. Due: 2026-10-04.
- [x] 2026-09-27 (session `grinder-ao-1` on kmaster) — **Done 2026-09-28 (Paul): wait for a reading; the person's own Promote press still goes through (the default, and the techlead's recommendation). Slice 2 writes it into §6.** Was: **Decide: should the automatic promote act when it cannot read which commit is live? Default if you say nothing: it waits for a reading, and your own Promote press still goes through.** The promote's readings and policy merged in #663 (TD-132 slice 1). It compares live with main; when a repo's `check` has never succeeded, live is unknown, which counts as not-main, so with `auto: true` it would start a run — bounded: one run, and if live is still not main the repo stops with a failure until you clear it. The techlead (reading #663) recommends waiting for a live it has read, with the press allowed through as it is through checks that are not green; design §6 is silent. Matters only once a repo carries a `promote:` block and you turn auto on, which slice 4 prepares for this repo; slice 2 writes the answer into §6 and builds it. (Put here because the person inbox was full.) Context: TD-132 / PR #663. Due: 2026-10-01.

- [x] 2026-09-14 (session 0675b93e `orchestrator-ao-1` run 3 on kmaster) — **Handled 2026-09-20 (`tdgrind-ao-1`, TD-090 in the archive): `translate()` reads `SessionStart`'s `source` and a `compact` changes no state, so an idle session stays `idle` through a `/compact`; merged, live check pending — the next `/compact` on a live worker should leave its card `idle`, not `stalled?`, after the next promote.** **A `/compact` leaves a healthy session reading `stalled?` forever, and §6's stall policy would wrap it up for it. No ledger entry holds this.** Observed live on `ao-agentorc-tdgrind-ao-1` at 14:58Z: state `stalled?` (confidence `scraped`), `ao explain` giving no rule and no evidence, `last_hook` 14:28:28Z — while the pane showed a **completed** `/compact` ("Compacted (ctrl+o to see full summary)", five files listed below it) above an **empty composer**. That session is `idle` in substance: every lane item done or dropped, tree clean, nothing running. **The mechanism, from the code:** `translate()` (`src/agentorc/adapters/claude_code/hook.py:43-87`) passes `SessionStart` straight into `STATE_EVENTS` → `working` (`hook.py:24-28`) with **no look at the payload's `source`** — and Claude Code's documented compaction sequence ends by firing `SessionStart` again with `source: "compact"` (`PreCompact` → `PostCompact` → `SessionStart(compact)`; the hooks guide's own "re-inject context after compaction" recipe matches on exactly that). Neither `PreCompact` nor `PostCompact` is in `HOOK_EVENTS` (`adapters/claude_code/__init__.py:30-42`), so nothing ever reports the compaction finishing; the record sits `working` until the liveness cross-check at `agent.py:556-559` flips it to `stalled?` at `STALL_AFTER`. I did not capture the 14:28:28Z payload, so *which* event set `working` is inference — but it is the only registered event the documented sequence fires, and `/compact` is a built-in that most likely never reaches `UserPromptSubmit` at all. **The shape of the bug is already solved next door in the same function:** `SessionEnd` is checked against `SESSION_END_STILL_RUNNING` (`{"clear", "resume"}`, `hook.py:31`) precisely because some lifecycle events are not what they appear — `SessionStart`'s `source` wants the same treatment, or `PostCompact` wants registering as the explicit "compaction over" signal, or both. **Why it is worth your time rather than a curiosity:** auto-compaction is routine for exactly the long-running unattended workers this system exists to watch, so every one of them will eventually read `stalled?` while perfectly healthy. Today that only misleads a human reading the Herd and an orchestrator following its brief (I did not nudge — the tail shows output and the session holds no open reference, so the prescribed nudge had nothing to say). But §6's stall policy is **"`working` with no output past `stall_after` → flag `stalled?`, nudge once, then wrap up"**, and when that becomes agent code it will nudge and then wrap up a session whose only sin was compacting its context. **Sharpened 15:32Z, and it is worse than first written: the mislabel does not clear itself.** At 15:32Z the record still read `stalled?` with `last_hook` unmoved at 14:28:28Z, even though `last_output` had advanced to 15:26:06Z on an otherwise unchanged screen. The reason is that the liveness check is a one-way door: `agent.py:559` is the only assignment of `stalled?` anywhere, and its branch is guarded on `s.state == "working"`, so once the record leaves `working` that branch is structurally unreachable and no amount of fresh `last_output` can bring it back. Two paths *can* clear the state, and neither fires here: any hook carrying a mapped state reaches `_apply_event` (`agent.py:574-594`), whose `set_state` is unconditional — `SessionStart`, `UserPromptSubmit`, `PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse`, `Stop`, `SessionEnd` and `Notification` would all clear it — and the screen fallback (`_screen_verdict`, `agent.py:552-555,566-571`) would clear it if a rule matched, since it is not guarded on the current state. But a session resting at an empty composer emits no hook at all, and `ao explain` answers `match: null, reason: "no screen rule matched"` because no rule covers an idle composer. So the mislabel is not transient: nothing the system does on its own corrects it, and in practice it clears only when somebody submits a prompt — a submitted prompt being the only one of those events an idle session is likely to produce. That is precisely the window a supervisor acts in, which is what turns a cosmetic wrong badge into a policy that would nudge and then wrap up a healthy worker. Needs your call on whether this becomes a TD (an orchestrator never files its own) and on the fix shape. Not acted on: the sub-agent that confirmed the hook semantics suggested adding the handler to this repo's `.claude/settings.json` — that is both outside my brief and the wrong layer, since agentorc's hooks reach a session as a generated `--settings` layer (design §4.2), never through a repo's own file. Context: `src/agentorc/adapters/claude_code/hook.py:24-31,43-87`, `adapters/claude_code/__init__.py:30-42`, `src/sessionorc/agent.py:548-559`, design §4.2 (hook → state table) and §6 (stall policy), Claude Code hooks reference (`PreCompact`/`PostCompact`, `SessionStart` matcher `compact`). Due: 2026-09-15.

- [x] 2026-09-14 (session 0675b93e `orchestrator-ao-1` run 3 on kmaster) — **Folded 2026-09-23 into the dev-cadence line below (Paul: file all three there).** As written: **The cadence check's `review` row, once failed on a merged PR, can never be cleared — the only honest response leaves a permanently red row, and my brief's "second fail on the same PR" escalation fires on it whether or not the worker did anything wrong. No ledger entry holds this.** Observed on agentorc **PR #128** (`ledger-board-tdgrind`, a one-line board append, merged 01:42:48Z): the row read `no cadence-review: comment on the PR`, I sent `tdgrind-ao-1` the single nudge the brief prescribes, and it did the best thing available — at 01:42:58Z it posted a note recording that the review had *not* happened before the merge rather than back-filling a claim that it had, then at 01:53:33Z posted the evidence comment itself, labelled `(post-merge)`, verified the board line's every claim against main, and stated that a board-line PR is not exempt and every future PR gets its comment before the merge. The re-check still fails, now with `review comment posted after the merge (2026-09-14T01:53:33+00:00 > merge)`, and it will fail forever: `row_review` compares the comment's `created_at` to the merge time, and GitHub stamps both, so no action by anyone can make this PR's row green. That is the intended design — §4's whole point is that a timestamp cannot be rewritten — but it has a consequence nobody has written down: **a missed review is an unclearable state, not a fixable one**, so the check's per-PR verdict and every window sweep (`--since 7d`, `/stranded-work`) will carry this PR as a fail indefinitely. The same shape as the immortal `derived` claim this orchestrator filed on 2026-09-12, arriving from the other direction — there, a claim that could never be retired; here, a fail that can never be cleared. That one has since been **fixed and closed** (TD-045, PR #126, merged by `tdgrind-ao-1` earlier in this same run: a claim that resolves to `done` at the merge is no longer immortal), so it is offered as the precedent for how this class of problem gets settled, not as a live sibling. **Why it needs you rather than a worker:** the worker's behaviour was correct and a second nudge would punish honesty — a worker that learns the row is the thing being graded will back-date or edit a comment to clear it, which is precisely the failure §4 exists to prevent. So the question is about the *detector*, your call: should a fail whose cause is in the past be expressible as **acknowledged** (a distinct status the sweep can skip, e.g. a follow-up comment the row reads), or should the window sweep report unclearable fails separately from actionable ones, or is a permanently red row the correct and intended cost of a missed review? Related: it is also the brief's escalation rule that needs the answer — "a second fail on the same PR escalates" cannot distinguish a worker ignoring a nudge from a worker that answered it perfectly, which is the only reason this line exists. Not nudged twice; the worker's own commitment ("every PR from this session gets its evidence comment before the merge from here on") is the forward fix and needs nothing from you. Context: PR #128 and its two comments, `scripts/check_cadence.py` `row_review`, cadence.md §4 and §7, docs/briefs/orchestrator-ao-1.md (escalation rule), design §4.8. Due: 2026-09-14.

TD-204 holds it, and the recommended fix (the baseline taken before typing) is a grinder build.** Was: **`ao send --wait` reported `prompt-stalled` for a prompt that in fact ran; no ledger entry holds this.** At 2026-09-12 ~13:44Z the first of four relay sends to `ao-samscrape-td377-enumeration` returned `prompt-stalled: ao-samscrape-td377-enumeration showed no activity within 5 s` and exit 1. It had not stalled: `ao explain` immediately afterwards showed the turn **done at 13:45:25Z** (`last_hook` 13:45:27Z, state `idle` by hook) with a 27 s answer to that exact relay text on screen. The window is `SEND_STALL_SECONDS = 5.0` (`src/sessionorc/agent.py:55`), and `rpc_send`'s wait leg treats *any* record revision change as "started" (`agent.py:873-877`) — so the 5 s is the budget for the first hook of the new turn to reach the agent, not for the tool to begin thinking. The session was a long one (its pane footer read 372.1k tokens), which is the obvious suspect for a first hook later than 5 s, but the mechanism is not measured. Relays 2-4 to the same session minutes later returned `idle` normally, so it is intermittent, not a broken path. **Reproduced 14:55:45Z on a second session, and the long-context theory does not survive it:** the nudge to `ao-agentorc-tdgrind-ao-1` (up about an hour, nothing like 372k tokens) returned the same `prompt-stalled … within 5 s`, and the prompt had run — the record gained `TD-025 done #91 source: declared`, which only that prompt could have caused, with the turn settling at 14:55:50Z. So this occurrence is proved by its *effect*, not by a screen reading, and both a fresh short session and a 372k-token one hit it. Two in eight sends. Why it matters more than a cosmetic error: `prompt-stalled` is exactly the signal `ao --skill` and the orchestrator brief tell a caller to act on, and the sanctioned action after one is to look and then decide again — a caller that instead retried would paste a second prompt into a live turn, which is the TD-027 concatenation failure arriving through the front door. Handled here by verifying every send against `ao explain`/`ao tail` rather than its return value. Needs your call on whether this becomes a TD (an orchestrator never files its own), and on the shape of a fix — a longer or adapter-supplied window, or `prompt-stalled` re-checked against the composer being empty before it is raised. — grinder-ao-1, 2026-09-27: the mechanism is found by reading `rpc_send`: the tool's own `UserPromptSubmit`, landing while the paste is still being confirmed, sends the wait down its busy branch, which then waits for a further turn. Filed as TD-204, with a third shape: the baseline taken before typing. Context: `src/sessionorc/agent.py:55`, `agent.py:855-880`, TD-016 (archive), TD-027 (archive), design §4.2. Due: 2026-09-13.

So the row works and its verdict is correct; what is wrong is that its input is the invoker's checkout, and an orchestrator or a `/stranded-work` sweep runs it out of whatever clone it happens to sit in. Fix shape already exists in the same toolkit: dev-cadence TD-030's reader half has `nudge_user_attention.py --fetch` read a merely-behind clone's board from `origin/<default>` rather than the local file; the `deploy` row wants the same treatment (or an explicit `unknown` when the tree is behind its upstream, which is honest and cheap). The fix belongs in **eyecantell/dev-cadence**, not here and not in samscrape, because the script is SYNC. Not nudged to any worker: the five fails all say `skaffold run`, which is your action and is already asked for by two samscrape board items due today (its 2026-09-10 and 2026-09-11 entries, both `Due: 2026-09-12`), with several older lines overdue behind the same un-run rollout — the workers cannot clear it and deploying is outside every one of their briefs. - [x] 2026-09-12 (session b9d7bd29 `orchestrator-ao-1` run 2 on kmaster) — **Folded 2026-09-23 into the dev-cadence line below (Paul: file all three there).** As written: **The cadence check's `review` row names the *last* `cadence-review:` comment, so a PR whose author discloses its post-review fixes is reported as having reviewed itself — it reads as a §4 violation by a worker that did more than §4 asks. No ledger entry holds this.** cadence §4 says never edit the review comment, and the `/cadence` skill (its parity half, §7) says to post a new one if there is more to say; samscrape's grinders do exactly that: an independent `cadence-review: SHIP · Sonnet · …` first, then their own `cadence-review: FIXED · Opus 5 (author) · … findings addressed` disclosing what they fixed before merging. `check_cadence.py` surfaces the later one, so the row's reviewer field reads `Opus 5 (author)`. Seen on samscrape #830, #833, #838 and #839 — three of the six most recently merged worker PRs, four of the last seven — each verified by reading the comments: every one had a genuine independent Sonnet review underneath. **Why it is more than cosmetic: I nearly filed a board line accusing tdgrind-2 of skipping independent review on #830, on the strength of that row, and stopped only because I read the comments first.** The weekly `/stranded-work` sweep reads these rows in bulk over a window (cadence §4, `--since 7d`) and has no such habit, so the next sweep over this window will see a run of apparent self-reviews on a repo whose reviews were in fact independent — a false accusation is the likely output, and the disclosure convention is what triggers it, which punishes the careful worker. Cheap fixes, your call: report the **first** matching comment (the review is by definition the earliest), or report every match and let the reader see both, or have the row name the reviewer from the earliest match while keeping the latest verdict. The row's real purpose — evidence existed, unedited, before the merge — is satisfied in every one of these cases either way, so nothing is failing today. Needs your call on whether this becomes a dev-cadence TD (the script is SYNC; an orchestrator never files its own). - [x] decide 2026-09-28 (session `grinder-ao-1` on kmaster) — **For your read, nothing to do unless you disagree: slice 4 of metered profiles changed four things the design round had decided for the node's link. The techlead accepted them when it merged #688.** (1) The frame from home to node carries the account's window sums, not a reading, because a reading's percent is over one profile's own amount. (2) The push the design sent on `hello` is now the reply to the node's first `spend` on each dial. (3) The `spend` frame gained `profile`, `prices` and `cursors`, and the prices are the node's own, because the home may not define that profile. (4) A profile's first read travels as cursors only, with no history, and a long batch goes in pieces cut at a line, because one frame cannot hold every turn of every transcript. Each has its reason in design §4.4 *Usage* and §4.4a, and a dated line in design-history under §4.4. If one should go back, say which, and it gets a design round. Context: TD-151 / PR #688. Due: 2026-10-05. Answers: Keep all four as built (default) | One of them goes back to a design round. Decided: Keep all four as built (2026-09-30). - [x] act 2026-09-23 (the anchor session on kmaster) — **Four findings to file as dev-cadence TDs (Paul, 2026-09-23) — no dev-cadence session was up to take them, so this line holds them until one is.** (1) A `review` row that failed on a merged PR can never clear (`row_review` compares the comment's `created_at` to the merge time), so a manager's *second fail on the same PR* rule punishes a worker that answered honestly — an *acknowledged* state the sweep can skip, or sweeps reporting unclearable fails apart from actionable ones (agentorc PR #128, 2026-09-14). (2) The `deploy` row answers from the invoker's clone (`row_deploy(pr, root)`), so a behind checkout turns `fail` into `na` (samscrape #791–#796, 2026-09-12) — read at `origin/<default>` as `nudge_user_attention.py --fetch` does, or say `unknown` when behind. (3) The `review` row names the *last* `cadence-review:` comment, so an author's later disclosure comment reads as a self-review (samscrape #830/#833/#838/#839) — name the first match. (4) `cadence_hooks.sh --session-start` runs `nudge_user_attention.py --report --due-only --fetch` over every repo in the roster — 71 items, about 4,200 tokens, at every unattended start, restart and seat fill (TD-118): scope it to the session's own board, without `--fetch`, when the session is unattended (`AGENTORC_SESSION` set and the launch layer says unattended), and keep the machine-wide view for `/attention`. The evidence in full is in this file's history at the three 2026-09-12/14 lines folded here. Context: dev-cadence `scripts/check_cadence.py`, `scripts/cadence_hooks.sh`, cadence §4, §5, §7; TD-118. Due: 2026-09-30. Closed: 2026-09-30 — filed as dev-cadence#TD-076 (findings 1 to 3) and dev-cadence#TD-077 (finding 4), dev-cadence PR #188.

- [x] decide 2026-10-08 (session 705e0395 on kmaster) — Which prompt-cache levers should agentorc take? Context: TD-378, docs/decisions/2026-10-08-prompt-cache.md. Due: 2026-10-15. Answers: Pin 1h, restart lapsed, trial 200k (default) | Pin 1h and restart lapsed only | None for now. Decided: Pin 1h, restart lapsed, trial 200k (2026-10-08). Closed: 2026-10-08 — done: ADR accepted; options 2 and 3 are TD-458 and TD-459, the 200k trial set in .agentorc.yml and read back by TD-460; TD-378 archived.
  - **Context:** You asked on 2026-10-07 whether Claude's prompt cache could save tokens. Eight days of agentorc's sessions show most of the cost is re-reading long contexts from the cache:
    - reads are 58% of the cost, writes 26%, output 16%
    - requests over 200k tokens of context carry 35% of it
    - a cache that lapsed overnight and was written again is 6%, mostly grinders woken by mail after hours idle
    - Claude Code already keeps the cache for an hour on your subscription, and that is the biggest saving there is
  - **Question:** Which of the three changes should be built or tried?
  - **Caveats:** How your subscription's weekly limit counts cached reads is not published, so the percentages rank the changes and do not promise a saving. A lower bound restarts grinders more often: each restart costs a re-read of the ledger and the files, and nobody knows how much until it is tried.
  - **Recommended:** Pin 1h, restart lapsed, trial 200k — the first two are small and safe, and the trial measures the third on your weekly window. **Otherwise:** the first two only, if you would rather not change how often grinders restart; none, if the weekly window is not tight.
